## 2. The Lane and the Measure

**Rules on the page:** Thursday drawn by the standing after three (first v twenty-fourth…); Friday and Saturday by
lot at the pole the evening before (Saturday's put back to nine for the sitting), winners against winners, no four
twice; placed by lanes won, then points for less against; marked by place. Lane rules as Book One and ch. 18: three
for the litter over your own hurdle, a down one, two halves a down, a point at the glass to the half; a change of door
declared to a count by the captain's voice. The Board's lane mast stands on the Graving floor (Noa, Polglase).

**Seven's lane four:** Kiva, Anwen, Jude, Pru; Perrin captains and declares from outside the pen ("standing out on
his healer's card").

| Day | Lane | Seven's declaration | Score | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thu 2 p.m. | v the Tarn, second four (16th after three) | Rowan, stance | **6–2** | Carry 1: the pass fails (no grips); Tarn down + glass, 0–2. Carry 2: Kiva sits on the litter's head (Perrin's "hen"); Jude two downs, glass, 3–2. Carry 3: Anwen walks the foot round the Tarn's planted girl; Jude down; **over on Kiva's and Pru's forearms** ("elbows"), 6–2. Tarn captain: Bridie Hebden's cousin. |
| Fri 11 a.m. | v the Ropewalk, first four | stance (carry 1), **Ember** (carries 2–3) | **4–5** | Carrow declared "*edge. Declared. Single*"; Lyle stance only, leaning from the floor (Anwen's teaching). Carrow strikes on **Treen's word "set"** (Kiva's ladder word). Carry 1: Kiva downed on the litter; 3+1 RW. Carry 2 (Pru's idea): carry on forearms so Treen has no hands to read; Anwen walks her wall away from Lyle; Jude downs Lyle; over, 4–4. Carry 3: Treen reads feet; Kiva and Pru call at once ("two places"); Jude freezes; Lyle leans the litter a hand over the chalk; glass to RW, 4–5. |
| Sat 10 a.m. | v Team Twenty-Eight | **Ember, declared, single, "and no change"** | **6–5** | Pru calls the pen; Kiva calls nothing. Carry 1: Dray on Jude's one-armed left; down; 0–4. Carry 2: Jude does not turn; Anwen walks round Bannock; Jude down; over on forearms; 4–4. Carry 3: **the exchange** — Kiva at the head, Dray two paces off waiting for "changing on three"; Perrin's mouth opens on the count; Kiva shakes her head; she bounds on Ember a pace and a half; **Dray downs her** (TT28 +1); Jude downs Marten (+1); Anwen's wall over the litter's middle; Kiva kneeling by the head (nobody touches a downed fighter); glass to Seven by a foot (+1). |

Seven's Lane: won two, lost one, **16 for, 12 against**, **ninth** (marks 16).

**The Lane on the page (top three and Seven's group):** 1 the Ropewalk first (won three); 2 Westerley first; 3
Holloway second; … 9 Seven; 10 Halden ("by two points"); 11 Old Four ("by one"); 24 a four of the sixth circuit
(lost three, four points). *Author's reconciliation, not on the page:* 4 the Tarn second (+10), 5 the Ropewalk second
(+9), 6 an unnamed four (+8), 7 Holloway first (+7), 8 an unnamed four (+5), 12 Westerley second (−1), 14 Twenty-Eight
(−1). Records and scores used: Ropewalk first 7–0, 5–4, 5–3; Westerley first 6–3, 6–3, 5–2; Holloway second 5–3, 6–4,
5–2; Holloway first 7–1, 6–3, 3–5; Halden 6–2, 5–4, 2–5; Old Four 6–3, 5–4, 2–5; Westerley second 5–4, 3–6, 5–4;
Twenty-Eight 5–3, 4–6, 5–6.

**The Measure, read Sat 8 p.m. (on the page):** 1 **the Ropewalk School, first four, 82**; 2 Halden License 80;
3 Westerley first 79; 4 Old Four 78; 5 Holloway first 77; 6 Holloway second 76; **7 Team Seven 69**; 8 Team
Twenty-Eight 68; 9 Westerley second 68 (**tie broken by the best single place on any board: Twenty-Eight first on
the Hunt, Westerley second third**); 10 the Ropewalk second 66; 11 the Tarn second 59. Check: 58+24, 65+15, 56+23,
64+14, 59+18, 54+22, **53+16**, 57+11, 55+13, 46+20, 38+21 (the Tarn second's 38 after three is the author's
reconciliation). Every other four is at most 58. **Seven: Bench 19 + Carry 24 + Hunt 10 + Lane 16 = 69; on the roll
by one mark over ninth.** Seven won one board outright (the Carry). No board struck.

**The roll:** the Ropewalk first, Halden, Westerley first, Old Four, Holloway first, Holloway second, Team Seven, Team
Twenty-Eight. "Licensed for the year… If the Board warrants a Barrel response and you go, you go by name, on the
Board's warrant and the Board's ash." "No four is obliged to go." Warranted Sunday (§3).

## 3. The hearing

**First sitting, Fri 18th, 7 p.m., the Salt Hall.** The Clerk of the Measure presides with two Board members (a heavy
grey man, spectacles on his forehead; a thin woman with ink on her cuff, who keeps the ledger). Clock: the Harbour
Committee's midnight deadline (to go to the magistrate in the morning); the Board rises at ten.
- The Chair (Ysra Crest) presents the three sheets in under a minute.
- Noa: **a fourth column, ash ("What the comb says")**; four lines rise together, faster from the fifth month;
  "I've drawn nothing between them… If the Board wants a line, the Board can draw it." Reads the returns (§7). A
  laugh from the gallery at the Holloway line; it stops.
- Halden's counsel asks the Board to note the claim against the Hunt's red bags.
- **Adlam's bench books** (the foundation's own): 2402 *48*; 2431 *48*; 2604 *50*; ninth month 2680 *55*; Sat 5th
  *57* (the harbour bank, by his watch). "The count isn't the foundation's. It's got quicker." Leaves them on the table.
- **Kellan's figures, under his own name:** combed at the gate 1st–16th: **1,106 tins, jars and bags; seventh reed sang
  on 1,102; silent on 4** (Seven's sealed medical; the Tarn's soot-cut jar; a Westerley healer's tin from before the
  thaw; the Board's reference jar). The Hunt: **412.4 combed; 371.3 red; 41.1 clean, all dry.** The claims ledger:
  371.3 claimed. The house's own index: **2391–2600, 109 drams, all from its own yards; 2680, Meridian district outside
  its yards, 1,175.1.** What they come to: "every dram of ash killed in this country, except the tenth that happens to
  be dry… by next spring it's every dram." **Counsel withdraws the request "for the present"; "Well done, Renn."**
- The Committee's chairman (a ship's chandler, gold watch). **Peverell:** her pumps will stop eleven minutes at the
  slack by her hand; thirty-six names on her fault paper, not hers; "I'll be the keeper who let it be tried." A foot of
  water in Ropery Row; "move the coal up a stair."
- **Hart**, under his own name: the hand ruined first; quiet at slack; the neaps walk with the table; the springs dawn
  slack does not walk ("near a quarter past six"). "The hand's the one that reads. The good one never did."
- **Mara, on her own name:** Wednesday at 6.09, thirty-six hands not hers, one jar of plain a cell, each its own count;
  "if I'm wrong… it's on my name." Needs **thirty-six jars**; none in Saltmere or Meridian.
- The chairman asks for Seven's tin. **Perrin refuses: "It's for a hand… I'd sooner the low town were wet than that
  word was."** The binding is honoured.
- **The Order's sister** (the gate sister; came on Wednesday's boat): the Master has Mara's wire of the 12th and is
  on the coast boat, arriving Saturday at five, to speak for the cellar herself.
- **The answer to the Committee (8.40):** "The Board does not stop banks. It does not keep them… The keeper keeps her
  house."
- **Kiva stands** (~8.42): she has had it, at thirteen, on Classification Day and on the ferry; the page in the
  Library press; asks the Board to open it. Anwen stands with her; Aske stands at the back; Perrin's hand flat on the
  bench. **The Board will sit Sunday at two in the Graving** and asks for the page, Annick, Personal (in the Reader's
  custody) and the Ferren book for 2604.
- **The Proprietor's letter** (received 8.56; Adlam had gone along the quay at 8.45): he asks to be heard on the same
  matter under his own name; cannot leave his case; the Board will go to him **Sunday at nine** (the Board, Kiva, Mara,
  the Chair, the foundation's counsel only).

**The commission, Sun 20th, 9 a.m., the Counting House long room.** Kiva takes **the far chair** by choice (pulse falls
on the stair; she does not touch the banister). The Proprietor's evidence (the thin clerk writes it): the window, the
fever; **"I dreamed that night… no roof… something in it. Paying attention." Eleven times since; the last on the 17th
of the ninth month, 2680, in the evening; always with a fever; never once since 2611 without his hand on the glass.**
Told nobody: "I was ashamed." He read Annick in the Library in **2610**; the **2611 settlement** followed from it. His
reading: *some go out, some go white*; step the country "so that whatever comes finds them lit." **"I'm sure of my
reading. I'm not certain I'm right."** He asks Kiva, through the Board, whether any lights were white. **Kiva answers
yes** (the ferry only; lower; "I'll not have it said I saw more"); she tells it because withholding it would be choosing
a side by leaving a thing out of a column. He covers his eyes with his right hand for a count of ten; left hand never
moves. "It doesn't prove me right… It proves you saw what… I saw." **The Clerk asks if there is anything else: "There's
one thing I've no word for… I'll not put a thing on the Board's table that I can't name."** Mara: it is not on her page;
"She's never told me it." Written: *A thing she has no word for. Not put on the table.*

**The Graving sitting, Sun 20th, 2 p.m.** The table on the pen's middle line. Seven on the second bench; Aske behind.
- **Thrale** comes down the steps at two; stands at the floating gate "with the sea at my back"; an hour, gone by three.
- **The page:** the Reader (**Lavinia Arkwright**, Reader since 2649) attests her wax (Monday the 25th of the sixth month,
  2680, eight minutes to two); Mara states it was written on the 29th of the fifth month at ten at night. **Kiva and Mara
  break the seal together** (Kiva's left hand on Mara's right over the Reader's bone-handled knife). Mara reads it aloud
  (text in ch. 40: high; roads; unlike lights, people; **notes, "lost her number at four"**; lights touching; one note;
  no sky, enormous, full, paying attention; every lamp answered; **"something small. It ticked. Once… the word she'd
  use for both."**). Noa writes *N.B.* and nothing after.
- **Annick, Personal**, read aloud by the Reader with the Master's leave ("Read it… so that it belongs to nobody. And
  don't make it anything"). Includes *Some go out. Some go white. One turns a colour I have no word for.*
- The Ferren book (Lumb's) read by the Reader; Peverell reads her house's 2604 page herself; Noa reads the returns and
  **the new Holloway return** (§7).
- The Proprietor's evidence read by the thin clerk.
- **Thrale testifies:** one door, a ward, since nineteen; **dreamed it three times: at nineteen, the night she took her
  door; in 2670, the night she was made Master; the 17th of the ninth month 2680, at her window over the river at twenty
  past two in the afternoon, with a fever gone by dark.** Told nobody. Her reading: "one door, and one quiet thing, and
  let nothing in that asks… I'd sooner a house where nothing answers." "The Order hasn't got [a position]. If it had, I'd
  have to resign."
- **The jars and the price** (§4).
- Thrale to Kiva: "I think you were counting the roads… a good thing to count, and a hard one to stop." Leaves by the
  seaward ramp at ~2.55; Kiva's doors stand; her heat comes up ("a hundred and two").
- **Mara for the Works and the Line:** "Whatever's coming, don't be one engine when it comes… That's not a reading. It's
  a fitter's rule."
- **The Clerk's finding:** records the dreamers (Annick 2446–50; the Ferren and Saltmere keepers, 2604; the Westerley
  keeper, 2604; the Proprietor, 2604 and eleven times; Thrale, three times; the Holloway lamp-man, 2680, name withheld;
  Kiva Rowan, at thirteen and since, "from a page that was shut"). "The Board can't say what was dreamed." **"What the
  Master called the sky unroofed. The Board will call it the unroofed sky… and it'll not say what it is."** (First
  appearance of the name.) **Warrants a Barrel response at the harbour pump house at the dawn slack on Wednesday the 23rd**,
  on the keeper's leave, under the Saltmere Company's officers, the Works' reader on the landing, the keeper's fitters
  on the cells, the Order's thirty-six jars at the stated price; any four on the roll may stand by name on the Board's
  warrant and ash; **no four obliged**. The prediction opens at noon Wednesday. The claim is for the Compact's court
  (Kellan's figures and Adlam's books sent tonight). Noa's columns sent to every pump house and lamp office, without a line.
- **The Chair writes the third line on the *Witnessed* page:** *Sunday the 20th of the third month, 2681. Put it on the
  table herself, under her own name, before anybody asked. Y.C.*

## 4. Plain ash for the Slack

- **Thrale brought thirty-six jars** on a flat iron-wheeled cart (stone jars in straw, grey seals with the two hands).
  The Order's count: 44 before she was Master; 41 when made Master; 40 after Seven's 1.2 (second month); **36 given; 4
  left in the cellar.**
- **Price said aloud (Graving):** (1) the house breaks its only rule that isn't about doors (never for a bank), and its
  book records "Given to a bank, by the Master, who broke the house's rule to do it, and said the price first… and that
  I'd do it again"; (2) four jars left for every hurt hand on the road south; (3) **the thirty-six cells never to be
  stepped again while the pump house stands, each its own count, not two alike** — written by Peverell in the keeper's
  book for 2604 (the page after the white lamps): *Received from the Order of the Even Hand, thirty-six jars of plain. In
  return, the harbour bank never to be stepped again while the house stands. Each cell its own count. Peverell, keeper.*
  **Signed by the Harbour Committee's chairman.** "Then they're given. Not brought. Given."
- The jars stand on the cart at the Graving's seaward end overnight Sunday.
- **Seven's sealed tin (2.0, W, plain): asked for by the Committee; refused by Perrin; unopened; bound.**
- Thrale's limits on the page: an hour at a time in the town ("a day's work"); lodges in the Company's swept watch hut
  at the Mole's end, on the sea side, no cell in it; stands at the floating gate with the sea at her back; grey by 2.50;
  leaves at 2.55. Kiva's doors lie down at forty paces on the quay (not at the Order's gate).

## 5. Kiva Rowan at exit

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; one door declared at every lane by her captain's voice (stance Thu; stance then
  Ember Fri; Ember "and no change" Sat). **On the national license roll for the year with Seven.**
- **No crossing this movement. No stack. No three-door. Three clean, unchanged.** Hand warm: Thu (4 mornings), Fri (5),
  Sat (6). **Aske said the price twice (Thu 7 a.m., Sat 7.15 a.m.): the seventh past the elbow, five nights' sulk,
  "not surprised by six"; one in seven days.** **The week's crossing is kept for Wednesday the 23rd** (nobody has asked
  for it on the page).
- **Right hand:** blisters opened at Thursday's lane (bled through the linen); re-wound; healing; written left-handed
  all movement. **Sunday: a burn of cold "peel by Sunday" not shown on the page.** The two fingers as before.
- **Fever, the third this year** (Sun ~2.50 p.m. to a little after eleven; 102 in the Graving, 104 at the height). **No
  dream. "Nothing came," said aloud; Aske writes *Fever, the third. Nothing came. Said so.*** and shows her. Card line:
  *The third this year. Two Glory masters inside six hours.*
- **Near the Proprietor:** pulse falls on the stair; far chair by choice; no banister. **Near Thrale:** doors lie down
  at forty paces (quay) and on the Graving bench; her heat held while Thrale was in the dock and came up when she left.
- **The disk:** cold — at the Counting House, in the Graving, through the fever. No fourth-column entry owed.
- **Yes/no log (a quarter to seven):** Thu a mooring ring in the coping by the Graving, yes; Fri the Salt Hall gallery
  rail, yes; Sat the rail at the Graving's lip, yes; Sun the bed rail in the loft, yes (written by Pru, capped fingers).
- **Her book:** Thu right-hand line: *Kept the week's crossing for Wednesday… If nobody does, I've kept it for nothing,
  and that's all right.* Jude's team line (Thu, Quiller's): *Rowan told us a thing of her own. We'll stand where she
  stands* — signed by the four and Kiva.
- **The dream: now public.** Told Thu night (Anwen, Perrin, Jude, Pru); Fri morning (Aske, Orla, Kellan); Fri night (the
  Board, the Salt Hall). **The ferry's white lights: told to the Board's commission at the Counting House, Sunday.**
  **The colour with no word: hers alone; "not put on the table"; Mara has never been told it.**
- Kiva's development beats on the page: the week's crossing kept; elbows; "she's not reading the lane, she's being
  read for"; the decision at the Graving wall; telling her four first; "one voice" (Pru's pen); losing the exchange by
  choice; "a four and a method, not three doors"; giving the Proprietor the white; the page heard without shame.

| Lens | Ch. | Words | What it shows |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Anwen | 33 | 1,154 | Thu 8 a.m., the Ropewalk: teaches Lyle to lean from the floor (moves Anwen half a pace, then a pace); Carrow walking on Treen's "set"; "If the lot falls that way tomorrow, she'll lean on your four." "I said I would." |
| Mara | 34 | 1,977 | Thu 10 p.m., the keeper's room: three training cells (own count; Peverell's "rug"; the bank's); twelve fitters; *Town* or *mine*; Hart's mate: "nothing under it but more of itself." Kiva knocks twice and once; Mara asks only "Who first?"; writes the two wires on fault paper. "Mine." |
| Kellan | 36 | 1,376 | Fri 4 p.m., Halden technical office: Halden's card (junior assessor, Fire's wage) with counsel's line "given through the foundation's counsel"; Pettit; Adlam will read his books; Kellan writes *No… under my own name… With thanks. K. Renn.* |
| Rhea | 37 | 1,108 | Sat, fourth tier, with Pike: "Cade… He'd his mouth open on the count. She shook her head." Rhea's advice rule kept to the letter, broken in spirit ("*Mine*"). Writes **Anwen's Graywater card**; will not write Kiva's before the 30th. |

Viewpoint (script count over sections): **Kiva 47,999 of 55,187 body words, 87.0%**; cutaways **7,188**.

## 7. Evidence

- **The returns** (the Board's wire of Sat 12th to 140 houses on its roll; Lumb's notion): eleven by Thu 6.50 — eight
  *nothing found*; **Westerley harbour pump house 2604** (stepped quay lamps white, town out half an hour; keeper's
  fever; "the roof was off"); **Tarnfoot works 2604** (four drums stood up together after three; no fever written);
  **Holloway lamp office, 17th of the ninth month 2680** (every lamp on the ring once at 2.20 p.m.; night lamp-man's fever
  two nights; "would not have it written… would be laughed at"). **Sun 8 a.m., Holloway again** (Noa wired the warden
  himself on Fri night and said so): the lamp-man asks it be written — no roof, nothing over, something attending, the
  ring's lamps white and out both; "if the girl can say it to a Board under her name he can say it to his warden under
  his"; name on the form, not printed.
- **The gate tape:** a hair on all eight pens at **12.52 a.m. Thursday**, nobody on the mast; Yeo's closing crew shut the
  lower Mallow tear at the bottom of the ebb **at 12.52 by her book**. With Wednesday's 12.44 (a stow turned): "Two is
  not a column." No line drawn. Polglase: "an old man who's begun to look."
- **Noa's fourth column: ash** (the comb). No line drawn, before the Board or after.
- **The Step:** 48 (2402), 48 (2431), 50 (2604), 55 (ninth month 2680), 57 (Sat 5th, harbour bank). Prediction unchanged.
- **Mara's page** (2680): "lost her number at four"; "It ticked. Once." Someone in the tiers said *four*; Noa *N.B.*
- **The tide clock:** used only — Hart, Fri 2.19–2.39 neap slack "twenty-three minutes behind it, like a dog"; on the
  23rd "it'll not." Unexplained.
- Not explained, not spent: what the dream is; why the count; what attends; the notch (no crossings this week); the
  hair; the held slack; the disk; the colour.

## 8. Draft boards (a card is not a seat)

| Card | Text (on the page) | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| **Jude** — the Saltmere Company (Yeo) | Closing crew under Yeo, Longshore and boats, Company hand's wage, housed, from the 1st of the fourth month; "Six months, and then we look again." *You asked me on the Ropewalk steps what it was for. Come and find out.* | Left up. "I'll answer after Wednesday." |
| **Perrin** — Halden Lift Yards | "The winner of the Carry… carry's wage, on the Proprietor's roll." | **Taken down: *No, with thanks. It was mine, the ladder, but it was one Tuesday. P. Cade.*** Pinned face to the board. |
| **Perrin** — the Municipal Works (Rusk) | *Cade, P., carry, to the trunk crew at the Brickfields, from the autumn, when his card is up.* | Left up; "I'll answer at home." |
| **Kellan** — the Halden Foundation technical office | Junior assessor, Proprietor's roll, Fire's wage, from the autumn; back: *given through the foundation's counsel, as the foundation's.* | ***No. I'll give my figures under my own name… With thanks. K. Renn.*** Sunday night he is holding **a white card with a Halden mark** in the loft; its contents are not on the page. |
| **Anwen** — Graywater (R. Sorn) | Doran Mace's ward line on the night van, her own place, ward-hand's wage, from the 1st of the fourth month; "Mace asked for her by name." | ***Yes. A. Pryce.*** Told Kiva first, plainly. |

Kiva's own offers: none (Rhea will not write hers before the 30th). Pru: none ("I'm on a sheet").

## 9. Team Seven at exit

- **Seventh of twenty-four, 69; on the national roll by one mark; warranted (as a roll four) for Wednesday.** Won the
  Carry outright.
- **Perrin:** captain from outside the pen all three lanes; declared "and no change" Saturday; **his mouth opened on the
  count in the exchange and he shut it.** Aske's card: *Lifted against the card at the spur, Wednesday the 16th. A
  fortnight from Wednesday, not from Tuesday. No plant, no lift, nothing heavier than a cup.* **Took Kiva's arm on
  Quiller's stair Sunday night against the card; stopped by Aske ("Cade"); Anwen took his place.** Refused the Committee
  the tin. On being envied: "It feels like Gower eating a pasty and counting."
- **Anwen:** taught Lyle to lean; walked her wall from the far end in all three lanes; **Graywater seat, yes.**
- **Jude:** **Aske's card: *Talley. Left forearm, frost, mending. The edge on the right fist only. Nothing gripped with the
  left till Monday*** (a stripe from the Carry's loop; will scar; stiff in the cold for a year). Fought one-handed;
  carried one-handed. Keeps the book. Company card up.
- **Pru:** **two caps torn on Thursday's poles (fingertips bled); re-capped.** "Elbows" (her idea, Thu and Fri). **Called
  the pen alone on Saturday.** "I'm not grateful. I'm only good."
- **The book (Jude):**

| Date | Entry | Held |
|---|---|---:|
| Carried, Thu 17th | | 6.26 |
| Thu 17th | Lane v the Tarn second: Pryce 0.12, Talley 0.09, Finch 0.10; Rowan own thimble | −0.31 → 5.95 |
| Fri 18th | Lane v the Ropewalk first: Pryce 0.17, Talley 0.12, Finch 0.13; Rowan own thimble | −0.42 → 5.53 |
| Sat 19th | Lane v Twenty-Eight: Pryce 0.15, Talley 0.11, Finch 0.12; Rowan own thimble | −0.38 → **5.15** |

  Check: 6.26 − 1.11 = **5.15.** On the page: Saturday's line, "thirty-one, forty-two, thirty-eight. A dram and eleven.
  Held, five and fifteen." The Thursday and Friday per-head splits are the author's reconciliation. **Sealed medical, W,
  plain: 2.0, unopened, bound.**

## 10. Other people at exit

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Mara Rowan** | Took the national risk on her name (Fri). Did not own Kiva's choice; sent the wires; broke the seal with Kiva; read the page aloud. **Has learned on the page that there is a white she was never told and a thing with no word that she has never been told; will not ask.** "Don't be one engine." Teaching the thirty-six (twelve fitters first). Still has **not touched a cell**. |
| **Orla Dane** | Told by Kiva (Fri). Asked the Chair to strike her line when no longer needed (entered: *on evidence*). **One true sentence each with Hart:** Orla, "I didn't write after the Bench because I was afraid you'd write back and tell me it was my fault, and it was." Hart, "I went onto that Bench with a door I knew wasn't ready, because I wanted you to see me do it." "I'll not say it's all right." "I'd not believe you." Nothing mended. |
| **Ysra Crest** | Answered the Proprietor on Sun at 5 a.m.: *The sequence is not to be asked of her… I set none.* Was the ward-holder at the 2660 Bench when Hart's hand went. Wrote the **third *Witnessed* line**. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Spoke under his name at the Salt Hall (Kiva asked him). The Orla sentence. Will teach the twelve at a slack. |
| **Peverell** | "The keeper who let it be tried." Signed the Order's price into the 2604 book; had not slept since Tuesday. |
| **The Proprietor** | Testified he dreamed it (2604; eleven times; the last 17th ninth month 2680); read Annick in 2610; "sure of my reading… not certain I'm right." Left hand never lifted. Did not relinquish the claim (the Slack's business). |
| **Clemency Thrale** | In Saltmere from Sat 4.50 p.m.; Mole hut; testified; broke the house's rule with the price aloud; jars given. |
| **The Reader (L. Arkwright)** | Brought the page, Annick, Personal and Lumb's book; attested the wax; read Annick and the Ferren entry aloud. |
| **Kellan** | Read his figures; card answered *No*; Halden second by two marks; held up two fingers to Kiva from the floor. |
| **Adlam** | Read the bench books against the house's interest; took the news to the Proprietor at 8.45 Fri. |
| **Halden's counsel** | Withdrew "for the present"; "Well done, Renn." Present at the Counting House. |
| **Pettit** | Kept Kellan in the lane; "Read it clearly." |
| **Noa Bexley** | Fourth column; returns; **wired the Holloway warden himself** and asked that it be recorded. No line. |
| **Selka Dray** | Read on the mast by her own request ("Declared. Single. Read."); beat Kiva in the exchange; "the fairest lane I've ever lost"; Twenty-Eight eighth on a tie broken by the Hunt. Sunday night: "Wednesday. If they ask the roll… I'll be there. On my feet." Not a friend. |
| **Juniper Carrow** | Edge only, declared, "and no change" (Sat, from Perrin), walking on Treen's word "set" (from Kiva), Treen's "far edge" (from Pike's sheet via Ivey and Coombe). Won the Measure. "If they try, I want to be on the wall." |
| **Maud Lyle** | Leans from the floor (Anwen's). Downed by Jude on Friday; nearly smiled at Anwen. |
| **Treen** | Calls Carrow's strikes; learned to read feet off Kiva. |
| **Coombe, Ivey, Yeo** | Page delivered. Ivey's copies made; Yeo wants Kiva at the Company yard "not a Lane night" (agreed; not yet held). Yeo had the Mole hut swept. |
| **Pike** | Hands close halfway. On the tiers with Rhea; "That's a captain." |
| **Gower (Old Four)** | Envies Perrin's two cards. |
| **The Tarn** | Thursday: first four won by a point on the glass; second four 6–2 to Seven; Bridie's cousin is the second four's captain; Bridie to stand on Friday (not shown). |
| **The Harbour Committee's chairman** | Ship's chandler; gold watch; asked for Seven's tin; signed the Order's price. |
| **The Mercury's young man** | Three pencils; Sat morning sheet and Sun late sheet. |

- **Sedge** (Twenty-Eight's Hold, a tall fair girl) and **Bannock** (Twenty-Eight's still door, very big).
- **Lavinia** — the Reader's given name (surname Arkwright is Book One's); Reader since 2649.
- Unnamed on purpose: the printer's boy; the Tarn's second-four captain (Bridie's cousin); the Board's two members; the
  Harbour Committee's chairman; Halden's counsel; the Holloway lamp-man and warden; the Westerley keeper; the sixth
  circuit's four; the girl in sea blue (Westerley second's Hold).
- Rejected during drafting: Hester (registered collision).

## 13. Departures from the packet and items for attention

1. **Length:** 55,218 words (`wc -w`, headings included) — inside 50,000–56,000.
2. **Viewpoint:** Kiva 87.0% (at the packet's upper bound), cutaways 7,188 (inside 6,500–8,500).
3. **Sections:** 39 sections, **31 internal breaks (~5.6 per 10k; mean ~1,415 words)** after joining 38 intra-scene breaks.
   Below the formula's ~8.7 per 10k because three hearings and three lanes are long continuous scenes, which the packet
   says to keep unbroken. For review.
4. **The hearing ran over two days and three rooms** (Salt Hall Fri; Counting House Sun 9; Graving Sun 2) because the
   Proprietor cannot leave his case, Thrale cannot enter a stepped house for long (and the two Glory fields must not touch
   before the Slack), and the sealed page had to come two hundred miles. The Measure was read on Saturday night between
   the sittings. Exit state as the packet: the Measure over; the dream public with no agreed meaning.
5. **"The Unroofed Sky"** first appears in the Clerk's finding (ch. 40) and the Mercury's late sheet; the chapter title
   was changed so as not to precede it.
6. **Kiva put the dream on the table by her own choice** (Thu night), and then **chose to give the white lights to the
   Proprietor's commission** (Sun) though they serve his reading. The colour stays hers.
7. **Thrale's dream on the 17th of the ninth month** was in the afternoon at twenty past two (the minute the harbour bank
   stepped); the Proprietor's was that evening; the Holloway lamp-man's that evening. Recorded, not explained.
8. **The 12.52 a.m. hair** coincides with a tear closing; with Wednesday's 12.44 (a stow turning). Two events; no line.
9. **Thrale's effect on Kiva's doors at forty paces** (on the quay) is stronger than at her own house; Mara's line
   "In her own house she'd nothing to hold against. Here she has the whole town." No effect on the bank is shown.
10. **Kellan's Sunday-night white card** (Halden mark) is shown and not read; left for Movement Six.
11. **Perrin took Kiva's arm on the stair against his card (Sun night)**; stopped by Aske. Carry forward.
12. **Jude's arm** is retired as a thread of uncertainty: Aske has seen it; carded till Monday.
13. **Formula measures** (sentence distribution, paragraph proxy, readability, progression and combat density,
    development cadence) were written toward and **have not been measured.** Only words, sections and viewpoint were
    counted, for the records.
14. **Not done:** the Slack; the prediction's opening; any crossing, stack or three-door; the Proprietor's letting go;
    the point of light; the hull; Rhea's last night; the 30th; Kiva at the Company yard for Yeo's crews.

## 14. Dates ahead

Mon 21st: Kiva weak after the fever; Jude's arm free to grip. Mon–Tue: the thirty-six taught (Hart at a slack; Mara
at the cells). **Wed 23rd: the Slack at the dawn slack (prediction 6.09); the Board's Barrel warrant; any roll four by
name, none obliged; the Order's jars at the stated price. Kiva's week's crossing kept. The prediction opens at noon.**
Rhea's last night on the van before the 30th; her card ends on the 30th; she will ask then and write only if Kiva says
yes. Anwen to Graywater from the 1st of the fourth month; Jude to answer Yeo after Wednesday; Perrin's Works card to be
answered at home.
